\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{{32}}\chi_{a}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}{H^{3}}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}H\chi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left({4\kappa{\nu^{2}}}\right){\phi^{2}_{a}}-2\kappa\nu H{\phi^{2}_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa{H^{2}}{\phi^{2}_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa\chi_{a}^{2}{\phi^{2}_{a}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mu^{2}}{\nu^{2}}

(4.2.1)

We see that the gauge fixing term,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rm{GF}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}G_{a}^{2}\left(A\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left[{{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}+\xi ie\left({X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right)}\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}-ie{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[{X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}{\left({X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right)^{2}}

(4.2.2)

in the effective Lagrangian has been designed specifically to remove the pesky two point mixing terms from symmetry breaking. We now are left to the task of evaluating the last term of (
4.2.2
). Recall the earlier result of (
4.1.40
) of

\displaystyle X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{\sigma^{a}}\chi=\nu\left[{i\left({{\chi_{m}}{\delta_{am}}}\right)+{\chi_{2}}{\delta_{a1}}-{\chi_{1}}{\delta_{a2}}-{\chi_{0}}{\delta_{a3}}}\right]

Taking the hermitian conjugate and finding the difference

\displaystyle X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{\sigma^{a}}\chi-\chi{\sigma^{a}}X_{\rm{M}}

\displaystyle=2i\nu{\chi_{a}}

\displaystyle\implies X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-\chi{T^{a}}X_{\rm{M}}

\displaystyle=i\nu{\chi_{a}}

(4.2.3)

The gauge fixing terms final form is given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rm{GF}}=-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}-ie{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[{X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}

(4.2.4)

4.3
Complete Lagrangian

We make the cosmetic change of relabeling
\chi_{0}\to H
then list the complete Lagrangian

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{{\rm{eff}}}}:=\mathscr{L}+{\mathscr{L}_{{\rm{GF}}}}+{{\bar{u}}^{a}}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial_{\mu}}+\xi{e^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}\right){u^{a}}+e{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}}\right){u^{c}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}\nu H{{\bar{u}}^{a}}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}\nu{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\chi_{a}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}{u^{c}}

(4.3.1)

with

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}:={\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}}+{\mathscr{L}_{H}}

(4.3.2)

the pion-rho Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}-{b^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}e{\varepsilon_{aij}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\phi_{j}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{i}}-{\phi_{i}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{j}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}{\varepsilon_{bjk}}{\phi_{i}}{\phi_{k}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{b\mu}}-\frac{{{\lambda_{4}}}}{8}\left({\phi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}}\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}{F^{a\mu\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}e{\varepsilon_{abc}}{A^{b\mu}}{A^{c\nu}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}-\frac{1}{4}{e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\varepsilon_{ade}}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{A^{d\mu}}{A^{e\nu}}

(4.3.3)

the Higgs-rho-pion Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}H{\partial^{\mu}}H-{\mu^{2}}{H^{2}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}{\partial_{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}+ie{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[{X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right]-\frac{1}{4}e{\varepsilon_{abc}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{b}}}\right)A_{\mu}^{c}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}e\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}H}\right)A_{\mu}^{a}+\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}{\nu^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}\nu HA_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{8}{e^{2}}{H^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{8}{e^{2}}\chi_{b}^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}-\frac{\lambda}{{32}}{H^{4}}-\frac{\lambda}{{16}}{H^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}+

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{{32}}\chi_{a}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}{H^{3}}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}H\chi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left({4\kappa{\nu^{2}}}\right){\phi^{2}_{a}}-2\kappa\nu H{\phi^{2}_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa{H^{2}}{\phi^{2}_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa\chi_{a}^{2}{\phi^{2}_{a}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mu^{2}}{\nu^{2}}

(4.3.4)

and the gauge fixing Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rm{GF}}=-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}-ie{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[{X_{\rm{M}}^{\dagger}{T^{a}}\chi-{\chi^{\dagger}}{T^{a}}{X_{\rm{M}}}}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}

(4.3.5)

5
Path to Calculations

5.1
Renormalization Transformation

𝕋
hus far we have constructed a Lagrangian which contains bare fields and couplings. Bare in the sense that they are not directly related to experiment. To complete the definition of the theory, we must show how these bare couplings and fields are related to the experimentally measured coupling and give meaning to the fields and couplings. This is done by renormalization
[
42
,
43
,
44
]
. Since the complete bare Lagrangian has many terms in it, we shall split up the calculation and deal with the self contained pieces. We begin with the bare pion-rho Lagrangian:

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\phi_{0}A_{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{\phi_{0a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{0a}}-m_{0}^{2}\phi_{0a}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}{e_{0}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}A_{0\mu}^{a}\left({{\phi_{0j}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{0i}}-{\phi_{0i}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{0j}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}{e_{0}}^{2}{\varepsilon_{aij}}{\varepsilon_{bjk}}{\phi_{0i}}{\phi_{0k}}A_{0\mu}^{a}A_{0}^{b\mu}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\lambda_{{4_{0}}}}}}{8}\left({\phi_{0a}^{2}\phi_{0b}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}F_{0\mu\nu}^{a}F_{0}^{a\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}M_{0}^{2}A_{0\mu}^{a}A_{0}^{a\mu}-\frac{1}{{2{\xi_{0}}}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{0\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}{e_{0}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{0}^{b\mu}A_{0}^{c\nu}F_{0\mu\nu}^{a}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}e_{0}^{2}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\varepsilon_{ade}}A_{0\mu}^{b}A_{0\nu}^{c}A_{0}^{d\mu}A_{0}^{e\nu}

(5.1.1)

The subscript
0
is used to indicate bare quantities. We make a redefinition of the fields and couplings in terms of the renormalized fields and couplings:

\displaystyle{\phi_{0a}}

\displaystyle:=\phi_{a}\sqrt{{Z_{\phi}}}\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle{m_{0}}

\displaystyle:=m\sqrt{{Z_{m}}}\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle{e_{0}}

\displaystyle:=e\sqrt{{Z_{e}}}

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle{M_{0}}

\displaystyle:=M\sqrt{{Z_{M}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{{\xi_{0}}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\sqrt{{Z_{\xi}}}}}{\xi}

\displaystyle{\lambda_{{4_{0}}}}

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{4}\sqrt{{Z_{{\lambda_{4}}}}}

Substituting (
5.1.2
) into (
5.1.1
)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\phi_{0}A_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{Z_{\phi}}{\partial_{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}-{Z_{\phi}}{Z_{m}}{m^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}e{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\phi_{j}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{i}}-{\phi_{i}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{j}}}\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}{Z_{\phi}}{Z_{A}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}{\varepsilon_{bjk}}{\phi_{i}}{\phi_{k}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{b\mu}}-\frac{{Z_{\phi}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{{\lambda_{4}}}}}{\lambda_{4}}}}{8}\left({\phi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}{Z_{A}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}{F^{a\mu\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}{Z_{M}}{Z_{A}}{M^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{Z_{A}}\sqrt{{Z_{\xi}}}}}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}e\sqrt{{Z_{e}Z_{A}^{3}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{A^{b\mu}}{A^{c\nu}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}-\frac{1}{4}{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}Z_{A}^{2}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\varepsilon_{ade}}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{A^{d\mu}}{A^{e\nu}}

(5.1.3)

The definition of the
Z_{i}
factors are given by:

\displaystyle{Z_{\phi}}

\displaystyle:=1+\delta{Z_{\phi}}\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle{Z_{\phi}}{Z_{m}}{m^{2}}

\displaystyle:={m^{2}}+\delta{m^{2}}\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle e{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}

\displaystyle:=e+\delta{Z_{A{\phi^{2}}}}

(5.1.4)

\displaystyle{e}^{2}{Z_{e}}{Z_{\phi}}{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle:={e^{2}}+\delta{Z_{{\phi^{2}}{A^{2}}}}

\displaystyle Z_{\phi}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{{\lambda_{4}}}}}{\lambda_{4}}

\displaystyle:={\lambda_{4}}+\delta{Z_{{\phi^{4}}}}

\displaystyle{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle:=1+\delta{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle{Z_{M}}{Z_{A}}{M^{2}}

\displaystyle:={M^{2}}+\delta{M^{2}}

\displaystyle\frac{{{Z_{A}}\sqrt{{Z_{\xi}}}}}{\xi}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\xi}+\delta\xi

\displaystyle e\sqrt{{Z_{e}}Z_{A}^{3}}

\displaystyle:=e+\delta{Z_{{A^{2}}\partial A}}

\displaystyle{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}Z_{A}^{2}

\displaystyle:={e^{2}}+\delta{Z_{{A^{4}}}}

and substituting (
5.1.4
) into (
5.1.3
), the Lagrangian will split into two pieces

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\phi_{0}A_{0}}}={\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}}+{\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}^{\rm{CT}}}

(5.1.5)

where
{\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}}
is of a form that resembles the bare Lagrangian
{\mathscr{L}_{\phi_{0}A_{0}}}

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}-{m^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}e{\varepsilon_{aij}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\phi_{j}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{i}}-{\phi_{i}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{j}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}{\varepsilon_{bjk}}{\phi_{i}}{\phi_{k}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{b\mu}}-\frac{{{\lambda_{4}}}}{8}\left({\phi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}}\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}{F^{a\mu\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}{M^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}e{\varepsilon_{abc}}{A^{b\mu}}{A^{c\nu}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}-\frac{1}{4}{e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\varepsilon_{ade}}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{A^{d\mu}}{A^{e\nu}}

(5.1.6)

and the new piece
{\mathscr{L}^{\phi A}_{\rm{CT}}}
is the counter term Lagrangian

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\phi A}^{\rm{CT}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({\delta{Z_{\phi}}{\partial_{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{a}}-\delta{m^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{e}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\phi_{j}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{i}}-{\phi_{i}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\phi_{j}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{A{\phi^{2}}}}{\varepsilon_{aij}}{\varepsilon_{bjk}}{\phi_{i}}{\phi_{k}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{b\mu}}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{\delta{Z_{{\phi^{4}}}}}}{8}\left({\phi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{A}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}{F^{a\mu\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta{M^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{{A^{2}}\partial A}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{A^{b\mu}}{A^{c\nu}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{{A^{4}}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\varepsilon_{ade}}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}{A^{d\mu}}{A^{e\nu}}

(5.1.7)

which contains all the
\delta Z_{i}
factors. The bare Lagrangian from symmetry breaking is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{H0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{H_{0}}{\partial^{\mu}}{H_{0}}-m_{H0}^{2}{H_{0}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{\chi_{0a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{0a}}-{\xi_{0}}{M_{0}}^{2}\chi_{a}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}{e_{0}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}\left({{\chi_{0a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{0b}}}\right)A_{0\mu}^{c}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{e_{0}}\left({{\chi_{0a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{H_{0}}}\right)A_{0\mu}^{a}+\frac{1}{2}{e_{0}}^{2}{\nu_{0}}{H_{0}}A_{0\mu}^{a}{A_{0}}^{a\mu}+\frac{1}{8}{e_{0}}^{2}{H_{0}}^{2}A_{0\mu}^{a}{A_{0}}^{a\mu}+\frac{1}{8}{e_{0}}^{2}\chi_{0b}^{2}A_{0\mu}^{a}{A_{0}}^{a\mu}-\frac{{{\lambda_{0}}}}{{32}}{H_{0}}^{4}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\lambda_{0}}}}{{16}}{H_{0}}^{2}\chi_{0a}^{2}-\frac{{{\lambda_{0}}}}{{32}}\chi_{0a}^{2}\chi_{0b}^{2}-\frac{{{\nu_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}}}{4}{H_{0}}^{3}-\frac{{{\nu_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}}}{4}{H_{0}}\chi_{0a}^{2}-2{\kappa_{0}}{\nu_{0}}{H_{0}}\phi_{0a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}{\kappa_{0}}{H_{0}}^{2}\phi_{0a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}{\kappa_{0}}\chi_{0a}^{2}\phi_{0b}^{2}

(5.1.8)

Redefining the bare fields and couplings in term of the renormalized fields and couplings

\displaystyle{H_{0}}

\displaystyle:=H\sqrt{{Z_{H}}}\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle m_{H0}

\displaystyle:={m_{H}}\sqrt{{Z_{{m_{H}}}}}\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle{\chi_{0a}}

\displaystyle:={\chi_{a}}\sqrt{{Z_{\chi}}}

(5.1.9)

\displaystyle{M_{0}}

\displaystyle:=M\sqrt{{Z_{M}}}

\displaystyle{\nu_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\nu\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle{\lambda_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\lambda\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle{\kappa_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\kappa\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}}

Reusing some definitions in (
5.1.2
) and (
5.1.9
), and substituting into (
5.1.10
) gives us

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{H0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{Z_{H}}{\partial_{\mu}}H{\partial^{\mu}}H-{Z_{H}}{Z_{{m_{H}}}}m_{H}^{2}{H^{2}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left({{Z_{\chi}}{\partial_{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}-{\xi_{0}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{M}}{M^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}}\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}e{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{b}}}\right)A_{\mu}^{c}-\frac{1}{2}e\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{H}}{Z_{A}}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}H}\right)A_{\mu}^{a}+\frac{1}{2}{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{A}}{Z_{e}}\sqrt{{Z_{H}}{Z_{\nu}}}HA_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}{Z_{H}}{Z_{A}}{H^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{8}{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{A}}\chi_{b}^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}-\frac{\lambda}{{32}}Z_{H}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}{H^{4}}-\frac{\lambda}{{16}}{Z_{H}}{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}{H^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}+

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{{32}}Z_{\chi}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}\chi_{a}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\lambda}}Z_{H}^{3}}{H^{3}}-\frac{{\nu\lambda}}{4}{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\lambda}}{Z_{H}}}H\chi_{a}^{2}-2\kappa\nu{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{H}}}H\phi_{a}^{2}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\kappa{Z_{H}}{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}}{H^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\kappa{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}}\chi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}

(5.1.10)

The definition for the
Z_{i}
factors are

\displaystyle{Z_{H}}

\displaystyle:=1+\delta{Z_{H}}

\displaystyle{Z_{H}}{Z_{{m_{H}}}}m_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle:=m_{H}^{2}+\delta m_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle{Z_{\chi}}

\displaystyle:=1+\delta{Z_{\chi}}

(5.1.11)

\displaystyle{\xi_{0}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{M}}{M^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\xi{M^{2}}+\delta M_{\chi\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle e{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}

\displaystyle:=e+\delta{Z_{A{\chi^{2}}}}

\displaystyle e\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{H}}{Z_{A}}}

\displaystyle:=e+\delta{Z_{\chi HA}}

\displaystyle{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{A}}{Z_{e}}\sqrt{{Z_{H}}{Z_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle:={e^{2}}\nu+\delta{Z_{H{A^{2}}}}

\displaystyle{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}{Z_{H}}{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle:={e^{2}}+\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{A^{2}}}}

\displaystyle{e^{2}}{Z_{e}}{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle:={e^{2}}+\delta{Z_{{\chi^{2}}{A^{2}}}}

\displaystyle\lambda Z_{H}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle:=\lambda+\delta{Z_{{H^{4}}}}

\displaystyle\lambda{Z_{H}}{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle:=\lambda+\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{\chi^{2}}}}

\displaystyle\lambda Z_{\chi}^{2}\sqrt{{Z_{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle:=\lambda+\delta{Z_{{\chi^{4}}}}

\displaystyle\nu\lambda\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\lambda}}Z_{H}^{3}}

\displaystyle:=\nu\lambda+\delta{Z_{{H^{3}}}}

\displaystyle\nu\lambda{Z_{\chi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\lambda}}{Z_{H}}}

\displaystyle:=\nu\lambda+\delta{Z_{H{\chi^{2}}}}

\displaystyle\kappa\nu{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{H}}}

\displaystyle:=\kappa\nu+\delta{Z_{H{\phi^{2}}}}

\displaystyle\kappa{Z_{H}}{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle:=\kappa+\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{\phi^{2}}}}

\displaystyle\kappa{Z_{\chi}}{Z_{\phi}}\sqrt{{Z_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle:=\kappa+\delta{Z_{{\chi^{2}}{\phi^{2}}}}

Substituting (
5.1.11
) into (
5.1.10
), the Lagrangian will split into two pieces

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{H0}}={\mathscr{L}_{H}}+{\mathscr{L}_{H}^{\rm{CT}}}

(5.1.12)

where
{\mathscr{L}_{H}}
is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{H}{\partial^{\mu}}{H}-m_{H}^{2}{H}^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left({{\partial_{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}-{\xi}{M}^{2}\chi_{a}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}{e}{\varepsilon_{abc}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{b}}}\right)A_{\mu}^{c}-\frac{1}{2}{e}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{H}}\right)A_{\mu}^{a}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}{e}^{2}{\nu}{H}A_{\mu}^{a}{A}^{a\mu}+\frac{1}{8}{e}^{2}{H}^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}{A}^{a\mu}+\frac{1}{8}{e}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}{A}^{a\mu}-\frac{{{\lambda}}}{{32}}{H}^{4}-\frac{{{\lambda}}}{{16}}{H}^{2}\chi_{a}^{2}-\frac{{{\lambda}}}{{32}}\chi_{a}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}-\frac{{{\nu}{\lambda}}}{4}{H}^{3}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\nu}{\lambda}}}{4}{H}\chi_{a}^{2}-2{\kappa}{\nu}{H}\phi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}{\kappa}{H}^{2}\phi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}{\kappa}\chi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}

(5.1.13)

and
{\mathscr{L}_{H}^{\rm{CT}}}
is the counter term Lagrangian:

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{H}^{\rm{CT}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({\delta{Z_{H}}{\partial_{\mu}}H{\partial^{\mu}}H-\delta m_{H}^{2}{H^{2}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left({\delta{Z_{\chi}}{\partial_{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{a}}-\delta M_{\chi\xi}^{2}\chi_{a}^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{A{\chi^{2}}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}{\chi_{b}}}\right)A_{\mu}^{c}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{\chi HA}}\left({{\chi_{a}}\overleftrightarrow{\partial^{\mu}}H}\right)A_{\mu}^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{H{A^{2}}}}HA_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{8}\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{A^{2}}}}{H^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+\frac{1}{8}\delta{Z_{{\chi^{2}}{A^{2}}}}\chi_{b}^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{{32}}\delta{Z_{{H^{4}}}}{H^{4}}-\frac{1}{{16}}\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{\chi^{2}}}}{H^{2}}\chi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{{32}}\delta{Z_{{\chi^{4}}}}\chi_{a}^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{{H^{3}}}}{H^{3}}-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{H{\chi^{2}}}}H\chi_{a}^{2}-2\delta{Z_{H{\phi^{2}}}}H\phi_{a}^{2}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{{H^{2}}{\phi^{2}}}}{H^{2}}\phi_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{{\chi^{2}}{\phi^{2}}}}\chi_{a}^{2}\phi_{b}^{2}

(5.1.14)

Finally the bare Faddeev-Popov ghost Lagrangian is

\displaystyle{L_{0\rm{FPG}}}

\displaystyle={{\bar{u}}_{0}}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial_{\mu}}+{\xi_{0}}M_{0}^{2}}\right)u_{0}^{a}+{e_{0}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{0\mu}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}\bar{u}_{0}^{b}}\right)u_{0}^{c}+\frac{1}{2}{\xi_{0}}e_{0}^{2}{\nu_{0}}{H_{0}}{{\bar{u}}_{0}}^{a}u_{0}^{a}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{\xi_{0}}e_{0}^{2}{\nu_{0}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\chi_{0a}}{{\bar{u}}_{0}}^{b}u_{0}^{c}

(5.1.15)

Only a single field redefinition is required for the bare ghost field:

\displaystyle u_{0}^{a}:={u^{a}}\sqrt{{Z_{u}}}

(5.1.16)

Substituting the appropriate definitions from (
5.1.2
) and (
5.1.9
), and (
5.1.16
) into (
5.1.15
)

\displaystyle{L_{\rm{FPG}}}

\displaystyle={{\bar{u}}^{a}}\left({{Z_{u}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial_{\mu}}+{\xi_{0}}{M^{2}}{Z_{u}}{Z_{M}}}\right){u^{a}}+e{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}}\right){u^{c}}+\frac{1}{2}{\xi_{0}}{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{e}}{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\chi}}}H{{\bar{u}}^{a}}{u^{a}}+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{\xi_{0}}{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{e}}{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\chi}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\chi_{a}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}{u^{c}}

(5.1.17)

We define the
Z_{i}
factors as

\displaystyle{Z_{u}}

\displaystyle:=1+\delta{Z_{u}}

\displaystyle{\xi_{0}}{Z_{u}}{Z_{M}}{M^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\xi{M^{2}}+\delta M_{u\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle e{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{e}}{Z_{A}}}

\displaystyle:=e+\delta{Z_{A{u^{2}}}}

(5.1.18)

\displaystyle{\xi_{0}}{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{e}}{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\chi}}}

\displaystyle:=\xi{e^{2}}\nu+\delta{Z_{H{u^{2}}}}

\displaystyle{\xi_{0}}{e^{2}}\nu{Z_{e}}{Z_{u}}\sqrt{{Z_{\nu}}{Z_{\chi}}}

\displaystyle:=\xi{e^{2}}\nu+\delta{Z_{\chi{u^{2}}}}

Substituting (
5.1.18
) into (
5.1.17
), the Lagrangian will split into two pieces

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{0\rm{FPG}}}={\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}}+{\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}^{\rm{CT}}}

(5.1.19)

where
{\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}}
is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}}

\displaystyle={{\bar{u}}^{a}}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial_{\mu}}+\xi{M^{2}}}\right){u^{a}}+e{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}}\right){u^{c}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}\nu H{{\bar{u}}^{a}}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\xi{e^{2}}\nu{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\chi_{a}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}{u^{c}}

(5.1.20)

and
{\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}^{\rm{CT}}}
is the counter term Lagrangian:

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{\rm{FPG}}^{\rm{CT}}}={{\bar{u}}^{a}}\left({\delta{Z_{u}}{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial_{\mu}}+\delta M_{u\xi}^{2}}\right){u^{a}}+\delta{Z_{A{u^{2}}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}A_{\mu}^{a}\left({{\partial^{\mu}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}}\right){u^{c}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{H{u^{2}}}}H{{\bar{u}}^{a}}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta{Z_{\chi{u^{2}}}}{\varepsilon_{abc}}{\chi_{a}}{{\bar{u}}^{b}}{u^{c}}

(5.1.21)

The bare field and coupling redefinitions (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.9
), (
5.1.16
) together with the
Z_{i}
definitions in (
5.1.4
), (
5.1.11
) and (
5.1.18
) is the Renormalization Transformation.

5.2
Feynman Rules

The Feynman rule will be computed for the specific example of the rho Green’s function/propagator to demonstrate the ideas required to extract out the Feynman rules for this Lagrangian. The Lagrangian for the rho is

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{A}=-\frac{1}{4}F_{\alpha\beta}^{c}{F^{c\alpha\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}\right)^{2}}

(5.2.1)

with the anti-symmetric property of the field strength tensor

\displaystyle F_{\alpha\beta}^{c}

\displaystyle={{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{\partial_{\beta}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}

\displaystyle=-F_{\beta\alpha}^{c}

(5.2.2)

We can manipulate the form of the Lagrangian as follows

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\left({{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{\partial_{\beta}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}\right){F^{c\alpha\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\left({{F^{c\alpha\beta}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{F^{c\beta\alpha}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}{F^{c\alpha\beta}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}+\frac{1}{2}{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}-\frac{1}{{2\xi}}{\left({{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left[{{\partial^{\alpha}}{A^{c\beta}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{\partial^{\beta}}{A^{c\alpha}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}+\frac{1}{\xi}{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{\partial^{\beta}}A_{\beta}^{c}}\right]

(5.2.3)

where we have used the anti-symmetric property of the field strength tensor. The action for this Lagrangian is:

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\int{{d^{4}}z\mathscr{L}_{A}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int{{d^{4}}z\left[{{\partial^{\alpha}}{A^{c\beta}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{\partial^{\beta}}{A^{c\alpha}}{\partial_{\alpha}}A_{\beta}^{c}-{M^{2}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{A^{c\alpha}}+\frac{1}{\xi}{\partial^{\alpha}}A_{\alpha}^{c}{\partial^{\beta}}A_{\beta}^{c}}\right]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int{{d^{4}}z\left[{-A_{\nu}^{a}\left({\partial_{z}^{2}+{M^{2}}}\right){A^{a\nu}}+A_{\nu}^{a}{\partial^{\mu}}{\partial^{\nu}}A_{\mu}^{a}-\frac{1}{\xi}A_{\nu}^{a}{\partial^{\nu}}{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int{{d^{4}}zA_{\alpha}^{c}\left(z\right)\left[{\left({\partial_{z}^{2}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\alpha\beta}}-\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right)\partial_{z}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}^{\beta}}\right]A_{\beta}^{c}\left(z\right)}

(5.2.4)

where we have performed integration by parts with the surface terms contributing zero. The two-point function in configuration space is defined as

\displaystyle{\Gamma^{\left(2\right)}}\left({x,y}\right)

\displaystyle:=\frac{{{\delta^{2}}}}{{\delta A_{\mu}^{a}\left(x\right)\delta A_{\nu}^{b}\left(y\right)}}iS

\displaystyle=i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({\partial_{y}^{2}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\mu\nu}}-\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right)\partial_{y}^{\mu}\partial_{y}^{\nu}}\right]\delta\left({y-x}\right)

(5.2.5)

and the Fourier transform of the two-point function is

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\left(2\right)}}\left({p,q}\right)

\displaystyle=\int{{d^{4}}x{d^{4}}y}{e^{-i\left({px+qy}\right)}}{\Gamma^{\left(2\right)}}\left({x,y}\right)

\displaystyle=i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({-{q^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\mu\nu}}+\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right){q^{\mu}}{q^{\nu}}}\right]{\left({2\pi}\right)^{4}}{\delta^{4}}\left({p+q}\right)

(5.2.6)

We are now left to the task of finding the Green’s function which is the reciprocal of

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}\left(k\right)=-i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\mu\nu}}+\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right){k^{\mu}}{k^{\nu}}}\right]

(5.2.7)

where

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}_{ab}^{\alpha\beta}\left(k\right)D_{\beta bc}^{\mu}\left(k\right)

\displaystyle={\delta_{ac}}{g^{\alpha\mu}}

(5.2.8)

and
D_{\beta bc}^{\mu}\left(k\right)
the Green’s function. We can extract out the isospin indices
D_{\beta bc}^{\mu}\left(k\right)={\delta_{bc}}D_{\beta}^{\mu}\left(k\right)
and substituting into (
5.2.8
)

\displaystyle-i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\alpha\beta}}+\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right){k^{\alpha}}{k^{\beta}}}\right]\cdot{\delta_{bc}}D_{\beta}^{\mu}\left(k\right)

\displaystyle={\delta_{ac}}{g^{\alpha\mu}}

\displaystyle\implies\left[{\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\alpha\beta}}+\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right){k^{\alpha}}{k^{\beta}}}\right]D_{\beta}^{\mu}\left(k\right)

\displaystyle=i{g^{\alpha\mu}}

(5.2.9)

We need to find a
D_{\beta}^{\mu}\left(k\right)
which satisfies (
5.2.9
). The most general tensor we can construct is a linear combination of
g_{\beta}^{\mu}
and
{k^{\mu}}{k_{\beta}}
with

\displaystyle D_{\beta}^{\mu}\left(k\right):=Ag_{\beta}^{\mu}+B{k^{\mu}}{k_{\beta}}

(5.2.10)

with
A,B\in\mathbb{C}
. Substituting (
5.2.10
) into (
5.2.9
)

\displaystyle\left[{\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\alpha\beta}}+\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right){k^{\alpha}}{k^{\beta}}}\right]\left({Ag_{\beta}^{\mu}+B{k^{\mu}}{k_{\beta}}}\right)

\displaystyle=i{g^{\alpha\mu}}

\displaystyle\implies A\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right){g^{\alpha\mu}}+\left[{B\left({{M^{2}}-\frac{{{k^{2}}}}{\xi}}\right)+A\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right)}\right]{k^{\alpha}}{k^{\mu}}

\displaystyle=i{g^{\alpha\mu}}

(5.2.11)

Since
g_{\beta}^{\mu}
and
{k^{\mu}}{k_{\beta}}
are linearly independent we just have to match coefficients with

\displaystyle A\left({-{k^{2}}+{M^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=i\quad\textrm{and}\quad B\left({{M^{2}}-\frac{{{k^{2}}}}{\xi}}\right)+A\left({1-\frac{1}{\xi}}\right)=0

(5.2.12)

which has solutions:

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\dfrac{{A\left({\xi-1}\right)}}{{{k^{2}}-\xi{M^{2}}}}

(5.2.13)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{i}{{{k^{2}}-{M^{2}}}}

(5.2.14)

The rho Green’s function/propagator is then given by

\displaystyle D_{ab}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={\delta_{ab}}{D^{\mu\nu}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{i{\delta_{ab}}}}{{{k^{2}}-{M^{2}}+i\varepsilon}}\left[{{g^{\mu\nu}}+\frac{{\xi-1}}{{{k^{2}}-\xi{M^{2}}}}{k^{\mu}}{k^{\nu}}}\right]

(5.2.15)

The counter term Lagrangian for the rho propagator is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}_{A}^{\rm{CT}}}=-\frac{1}{4}\delta{Z_{A}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}{F^{a\mu\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta{M^{2}}A_{\mu}^{a}{A^{a\mu}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi{\left({{\partial^{\mu}}A_{\mu}^{a}}\right)^{2}}

(5.2.16)

Going through the above procedure again for the counter term Lagrangian but stopping at (
5.2.7
), we get the counter term for the rho two point function

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}_{ab\rm{CT}}^{\mu\nu}=i\delta_{ab}\left[\left(-k^{2}\delta Z_{A}+\delta M^{2}\right)g^{\mu\nu}-\left(\delta\xi-\delta Z_{A}\right)k^{\mu}k^{\nu}\right]

(5.2.17)

Part II
Quantum Corrections

6
Scattering Lengths at Tree Level

6.1
Useful Formulae

ℂ
onsider an on-shell scattering process of

\displaystyle\phi^{a}\left(p_{1}\right)+\phi^{b}\left(p_{2}\right)\to\phi^{c}\left(p_{3}\right)+\phi^{d}\left(p_{4}\right)

with the 4-momentum conservation equation

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu}+p_{2}^{\mu}=p_{3}^{\mu}+p_{4}^{\mu}

Since these particle are on-shell
p_{i}^{2}=m^{2}
. We can define the Mandlestam invariants:

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle:={\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)^{2}}={\left({{p_{3}}+{p_{4}}}\right)^{2}}

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle:={\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{3}}}\right)^{2}}={\left({{p_{4}}-{p_{2}}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle:={\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{4}}}\right)^{2}}={\left({{p_{3}}-{p_{2}}}\right)^{2}}

We can rewrite the dot products
p_{1}\cdot p_{2}
and
p_{3}\cdot p_{4}
using the
s
Mandlestam variable,

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=p_{1}^{2}+2{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}+p_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=2m^{2}+2{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{s-2m^{2}}}{2}

(6.1.2)

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=p_{3}^{2}+2{p_{3}}\cdot{p_{4}}+p_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{p_{3}}\cdot{p_{4}}

\displaystyle={p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}=\frac{{s-2m^{2}}}{2}

(6.1.3)

A summary of all the dot products are listed below

\displaystyle{p_{3}}\cdot{p_{4}}

\displaystyle={p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}=\frac{{s-2m^{2}}}{2}

(6.1.4)

\displaystyle{p_{2}}\cdot{p_{4}}

\displaystyle={p_{1}}\cdot{p_{3}}=\frac{{2m^{2}-t}}{2}

\displaystyle{p_{2}}\cdot{p_{3}}

\displaystyle={p_{1}}\cdot{p_{4}}=\frac{{2m^{2}-u}}{2}

Consider now the case of the elastic pion scattering process in the center of mass frame, with the incoming three momentum
\underline{q}
and the outgoing three momentum
\underline{q^{\prime}}
with

\displaystyle\underline{q}

\displaystyle\neq\underline{q}^{\prime}

(6.1.5)

\displaystyle q:=|\underline{q}|

\displaystyle=|\underline{q}^{\prime}|

(6.1.6)

where
q
is defined as the magnitude
\underline{q}
. For the
t
channel process,

Figure 6.1:
t
channel scattering process

Let the 3-momenta for
\phi^{a}
and
\phi^{c}
be
\underline{q}
and
\underline{q}^{\prime}
respectively, such that

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\left({{E_{{\underline{q}}}},\underline{q}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}},\underline{q}}\right)

(6.1.7)

\displaystyle{p_{3}^{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\left({{E_{{\underline{q}^{\prime}}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q^{\prime}}{|^{2}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)

(6.1.8)

The product of
p_{1}\cdot p_{3}
is

\displaystyle{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{3}}

\displaystyle={\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}}}\right)^{2}}-\underline{q}\cdot\underline{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}-|\underline{q}||\underline{q^{\prime}}|\cos\theta

\displaystyle=m^{2}+{q^{2}}\left({1-\cos\theta}\right)

(6.1.9)

Substituting
{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{3}}
from (
6.1.4
) into (
6.1.9
) the
t
Mandlestam variable is now given by

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle=2m^{2}-2{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{3}}

\displaystyle=2m^{2}-2\left[{m^{2}+{q^{2}}\left({1-\cos\theta}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=-2{q^{2}}\left({1-\cos\theta}\right)

(6.1.10)

Consider the
u
channel scattering process.

Figure 6.2:
u
channel scattering process

Let the 3-momenta for
\phi^{a}
and
\phi^{d}
be
\underline{q}
and
\underline{q}^{\prime}
respectively, such that

\displaystyle{p_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({{E_{{\underline{q}}}},\underline{q}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}},\underline{q}}\right)

(6.1.11)

\displaystyle{p_{4}}

\displaystyle=\left({{E_{{\underline{q}^{\prime}}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q^{\prime}}{|^{2}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)=\left({\sqrt{m^{2}+|\underline{q}{|^{2}}},\underline{q^{\prime}}}\right)

(6.1.12)

The product of
p_{1}\cdot p_{4}
is

\displaystyle{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{4}}

\displaystyle=m^{2}+{q^{2}}\left({1+\cos\theta}\right)

(6.1.13)

Substituting
{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{4}}
from (
6.1.4
) into (
6.1.13
) the
u
Mandlestam variable is now given by

\displaystyle u=-2{q^{2}}\left({1+\cos\theta}\right)

(6.1.14)

The three Mandlestam variables are related to each with

\displaystyle s+t+u=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{4}{p_{j}^{2}}=4m^{2}

(6.1.15)

We can use (
6.1.15
) to find the
s
Mandlestam variable from
t
and
u
.

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=4m^{2}-t-u

\displaystyle=4m^{2}+2{q^{2}}\left({1-\cos\theta}\right)+2{q^{2}}\left({1+\cos\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=4m^{2}+4{q^{2}}

(6.1.16)

We shall define the cosine of the scattering angle and the ratio
R

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle:=\cos\theta

(6.1.17)

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle:=\frac{q^{2}}{m^{2}}

(6.1.18)

Then the necessary formulae for the following calculations in terms of
z
and
R
are

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=4{m^{2}}\left(1+R\right)

(6.1.19)

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle=-2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R

(6.1.20)

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=-2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R

(6.1.21)

\displaystyle s-t

\displaystyle=2m^{2}\left[2+\left(3-z\right)R\right]

(6.1.22)

\displaystyle s-u

\displaystyle=2m^{2}\left[2+\left(3+z\right)R\right]

(6.1.23)

\displaystyle t-u

\displaystyle=4{m^{2}}Rz

(6.1.24)

6.3
Isospin Amplitudes

The most general form of the scattering amplitude is

\displaystyle{M^{ab,cd}}=F\left({s,t,u}\right){\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}+G\left({s,t,u}\right){\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}+H\left({s,t,u}\right){\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}

(6.3.1)

The amplitude can be decomposed over an isospin invariant basis

\displaystyle{M^{ab,cd}}=\sum\limits_{m=0}^{2}{{T^{m}}P_{m}^{abcd}}

(6.3.2)

where
T^{m}
are the isospin amplitudes and the basis vectors
[
45
]
are

\displaystyle P_{0}^{abcd}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{3}{\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}

(6.3.3)

\displaystyle P_{1}^{abcd}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}-{\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}}\right)

(6.3.4)

\displaystyle P_{2}^{abcd}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left({{\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}+{\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}}\right)-\frac{1}{3}{\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}

(6.3.5)

Expanding the amplitude (
6.3.2
)

\displaystyle{M^{ab,cd}}=\frac{1}{3}\left({{T^{0}}-{T^{2}}}\right){\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}+\frac{1}{2}\left({{T^{1}}+{T^{2}}}\right){\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}-\frac{1}{2}\left({{T^{1}}-{T^{2}}}\right){\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}

(6.3.6)

and identifying coefficients between (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.6
), we obtain a system of linear equations

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left({{T^{0}}-{T^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=F\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left({{T^{1}}+{T^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=G\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.8)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left({{T^{1}}-{T^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=H\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.9)

which has the solution,

\displaystyle{T^{0}}

\displaystyle=3F\left({s,t,u}\right)+G\left({s,t,u}\right)+H\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.10)

\displaystyle{T_{1}}

\displaystyle=G\left({s,t,u}\right)-H\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.11)

\displaystyle{T_{2}}

\displaystyle=G\left({s,t,u}\right)+H\left({s,t,u}\right)

(6.3.12)

The isospin amplitudes for rho mediation are given by:

\displaystyle T_{A}^{0}

\displaystyle:=3\left({{T_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)-\left({{S_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)+\left({{S_{A}}-{T_{A}}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left({{T_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)

\displaystyle=4i{e^{2}}{m^{2}}\left[{\frac{{2+\left({3+z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}+\frac{{2+\left({3-z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}}\right]

(6.3.13)

\displaystyle T_{A}^{1}

\displaystyle:=-\left({{S_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)-\left({{S_{A}}-{T_{A}}}\right)

\displaystyle=-2{S_{A}}+{T_{A}}-{U_{A}}

\displaystyle=2i{e^{2}}{m^{2}}\left[{\frac{{4zR}}{{4{m^{2}}\left({1+R}\right)-{M^{2}}}}+\frac{{2+\left({3+z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}-\frac{{2+\left({3-z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}}\right]

(6.3.14)

\displaystyle T_{A}^{2}

\displaystyle:=-\left({{S_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)+\left({{S_{A}}-{T_{A}}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left({{T_{A}}+{U_{A}}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}T_{A}^{0}

(6.3.15)

The isospin amplitudes for Higgs mediator are given by:

\displaystyle T_{H}^{0}

\displaystyle:=3{S_{H}}+{T_{H}}+{U_{H}}

\displaystyle=-3i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{s-m_{H}^{2}}}-i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{t-m_{H}^{2}}}-i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{u-m_{H}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[-{\frac{3}{{4{m^{2}}\left({1+R}\right)-m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]

(6.3.16)

\displaystyle T_{H}^{1}

\displaystyle:={T_{H}}-{U_{H}}

\displaystyle=-i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{t-m_{H}^{2}}}+i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{u-m_{H}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]

(6.3.17)

\displaystyle T_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle:={T_{H}}+{U_{H}}

\displaystyle=-i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{t-m_{H}^{2}}}-i\frac{{16{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{{u-m_{H}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]

(6.3.18)

The isospin amplitudes for the four pion vertex are given by:

\displaystyle T_{\lambda}^{0}

\displaystyle:=-5i{\lambda_{4}}

\displaystyle T_{\lambda}^{1}

\displaystyle:=0

\displaystyle T_{\lambda}^{2}

\displaystyle:=-2i{\lambda_{4}}

(6.3.19)

The sum of the amplitudes of the Higgs and 4 pion tree diagrams are:

\displaystyle T_{H\lambda}^{0}

\displaystyle=T_{H}^{0}+T_{\lambda}^{0}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{-\frac{3}{{4{m^{2}}\left({1+R}\right)-m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]-5i{\lambda_{4}}

(6.3.20)

\displaystyle T_{H\lambda}^{1}

\displaystyle=T_{H}^{1}+T_{\lambda}^{1}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]

(6.3.21)

\displaystyle T_{H\lambda}^{2}

\displaystyle=T_{H}^{2}+T_{\lambda}^{2}

\displaystyle=16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]-2i{\lambda_{4}}

(6.3.22)

6.4
Scattering Lengths

Scattering lengths are computed from the coefficients of the partial wave scattering amplitude with the partial wave scattering
amplitude obtained from the projection of the isospin amplitudes over the Legendre polynomials. We begin with the isospin amplitude

{T^{I}}\left({{q^{2}},z}\right)
expressed as a sum over partial scattering amplitudes
T_{m}^{I}\left(q^{2}\right)

\displaystyle{T^{I}}\left({{q^{2}},z}\right)=32\pi\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}{\left({2m+1}\right){P_{m}}\left(z\right)T_{m}^{I}\left({{q^{2}}}\right)}

(6.4.1)

where
{P_{m}}\left(z\right)
is the Legendre polynomials indexed by
m
with
z=\cos\theta
as defined in (
6.1.17
). Using the orthogonality of the Legendre polynomials on the inner product:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-1}^{1}{{P_{m}}\left(z\right){P_{n}}\left(z\right)dz}=\frac{2}{{2m+1}}{\delta_{mn}}

(6.4.2)

the partial scattering amplitudes can be extracted by multiplying
{T^{I}}\left({{q^{2}},z}\right)
by the Legendre polynomials and integrating:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-1}^{1}{{P_{n}}\left(z\right){T^{I}}\left({{q^{2}},z}\right)dz}

\displaystyle=32\pi\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}{\left({2m+1}\right)\int\limits_{-1}^{1}{{P_{m}}\left(z\right){P_{n}}\left(z\right)dz}\cdot T_{m}^{I}\left({{q^{2}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=32\pi\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}{\left({2m+1}\right)\frac{2}{{2m+1}}{\delta_{mn}}\cdot T_{m}^{I}\left({{q^{2}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=64\pi T_{n}^{I}\left({{q^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\implies T_{n}^{I}\left({{q^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{64\pi}}\int\limits_{-1}^{1}{{P_{n}}\left(z\right){T^{I}}\left({{q^{2}},z}\right)dz}

(6.4.3)

A Maclaurin series expansion can be made in terms of
q^{2}

[
46
]
as

\displaystyle T_{n}^{I}

\displaystyle=i{\left({\frac{{{q^{2}}}}{{m_{\pi}^{2}}}}\right)^{n}}\left[{a_{n}^{I}+b_{n}^{I}\left({\frac{{{q^{2}}}}{{m_{\pi}^{2}}}}\right)+\ldots}\right]

\displaystyle=iR^{n}\left[{a_{n}^{I}+b_{n}^{I}R+\ldots}\right]

(6.4.4)

where
R=\dfrac{q^{2}}{m^{2}}
which was defined in (
6.1.18
) and
a_{n}^{I}
and
b_{n}^{I}
are the scattering lengths. We shall project the isospin amplitudes using the first three Legendre polynomials which are

\displaystyle{P_{0}}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle{P_{1}}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=z

\displaystyle{P_{2}}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left({3{z^{2}}-1}\right)

(6.4.5)

The first two scattering lengths are calculated below. For
T^{0}

\displaystyle{T^{0}}\left(R\right)

\displaystyle=T_{A}^{0}+T_{H\lambda}^{0}

\displaystyle=4i{e^{2}}{m^{2}}\left[{\frac{{2+\left({3+z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}+\frac{{2+\left({3-z}\right)R}}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+{M^{2}}}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+16i{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left[{-\frac{3}{{4{m^{2}}\left({1+R}\right)-m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1-z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{1}{{2{m^{2}}\left({1+z}\right)R+m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]

(6.4.6)

The partial wave amplitude
T_{0}^{0}
is

\displaystyle T_{0}^{0}\left(R\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{64\pi}}\int\limits_{-1}^{1}{{P_{0}}\left(z\right){T^{0}}\left({R,z}\right)dz}

\displaystyle=a_{0}^{0}+b_{0}^{0}R

(6.4.7)

Setting
R=0
gives us

\displaystyle a_{0}^{0}

\displaystyle={\left.{T_{0}^{0}\left(R\right)}\right|_{R=0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{2\pi}}\frac{{{m^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}+\frac{{{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{\pi}\left[{\frac{1}{{m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{3}{{2\left({4{m^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)}}}\right]-\frac{{5{\lambda_{4}}}}{{32\pi}}

(6.4.8)

and differentiating with respect to
R
and then setting to zero

\displaystyle b_{0}^{0}

\displaystyle={\left.{\frac{{dT_{0}^{0}\left(R\right)}}{{dR}}}\right|_{R=0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{4\pi}}\frac{{{m^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}\left({3-4\frac{{{m^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}}\right)+\frac{{2{m^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}}}{\pi}\left[{\frac{3}{{{{\left({4{m^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)}^{2}}}}-\frac{1}{{m_{H}^{4}}}}\right]

(6.4.9)

The above calculations can be repeated for
T^{1},T^{2}
and the three Legendre polynomials
P_{0}(z),P_{1}(z)
and
P_{2}(z)
.

7
One Loop Corrections

𝕋
he tree results for the scattering lengths can be improved upon by including in the next order correction terms from the pertubative series. This can be done systematically by first computing the one particle irreducible (1PI) diagrams for the self energies and verticies. These 1PI diagrams then serve as the building blocks for the higher order analysis
[
54
]
. We shall list the topologies that contribute to the self energies and verticies. In total there are
\sim 85
diagrams.

7.1
One Loop Topologies

Diagrams that contribute to the self energy of the rho.

Diagrams that contribute to the self energy of the Higgs.

The lollipop diagrams for the rho and Higgs self energies will be included separately later during the analysis.
Diagrams that contribute to the vertex of the
\phi\phi\rho
.

Diagrams that contribute to the vertex of the
\phi\phi H
.

Diagrams that contribute to the
\phi\phi
scattering via a loop.

7.2
Dimensional Regularization

To parameterize the divergences that result in higher order loop calculations, we shall use dimensional regularization
[
55
]
. This is done by analytically continuing the spacetime dimension from
n=4
dimensions to
n=4-2\varepsilon
dimensions
[
56
]
. Making this change in the spacetime dimension will capture the divergences in terms of the form
\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon}
and
\dfrac{p^{2}}{\varepsilon}
which can be appropriately absorbed by the counter terms. A further consequence of changing the spacetime dimension to
n=4-2\varepsilon
is that the couplings are not dimensionless. This can be remedied by explicitly taking out the extra mass dimension. The action in
n
dimensions is

\displaystyle S=\int{{d^{n}}x\mathscr{L}}

(7.2.1)

Since the action is dimensionless

\displaystyle\left[S\right]=1\implies\left[{{d^{n}}x}\right]={\left[{dx}\right]^{n}}={M^{-n}}\text{ and }\left[\mathscr{L}\right]={M^{n}}

(7.2.2)

Using the dimension of the Lagrangian we can determine the dimensions of the fields:

\displaystyle\left[A\right]=\left[\phi\right]=\left[\chi\right]=\left[H\right]=M^{\frac{n-2}{2}}

(7.2.3)

The dimensionful couplings can now be worked out to be

\displaystyle\left[e\right]={M^{\frac{{4-n}}{2}}},\quad\left[{{\lambda_{4}}}\right]={M^{4-n}},\quad\left[\nu\right]={M^{\frac{{n-4}}{2}}},\quad\left[\lambda\right]={M^{4-n}},\quad\left[\kappa\right]={M^{4-n}}

(7.2.4)

We shall define a parameter
\mu
which is referred to as the renormalization scale with dimensions of mass,
\left[\mu\right]=M
and define dimensionless couplings in terms of the renormalization scale as

\displaystyle e\sim{\mu^{\frac{{4-n}}{2}}}e,\quad{\lambda_{4}}\sim{\mu^{4-n}}{\lambda_{4}},\quad\nu\sim{\mu^{\frac{{n-4}}{2}}}\nu,\quad\lambda\sim{\mu^{4-n}}\lambda,\quad\kappa\sim{\mu^{4-n}}\kappa

(7.2.5)

We will suppress the explicit appearance of the scale during the calculations but absorb it into the definition of the Passarino-Veltman scalar functions.

7.3
Passarino-Veltman Reduction

An efficient way to compute the one loop diagrams is to use
the Passarino-Veltman reduction technique
[
57
]
. This technique allows us to compute a Feynman
diagram in terms of a few master scalar integrals
[
58
,
59
]
, which in principle only have to be
computed once. The Passarino-Veltman reduction technique is implemented by using the denominators of the propagators
to write all the scalar products between external momenta and the loop momentum. Practically this means expressing
the
numerators of the Feynman amplitude in terms of the denominator, leaving only scalar integrals to be evaluated. This is demonstrated
below for the case of a vector self energy diagram.

The Feynman verticies are:

\displaystyle{\Gamma_{1}}=e{\varepsilon_{adc}}{\left({2k+p}\right)^{\mu}}\qquad{\Gamma_{2}}=e{\varepsilon_{bef}}{\left({2k+p}\right)^{\nu}}

with the self energy contribution due the pion bubble:

\displaystyle-i\pi_{ab1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}{\Gamma_{1}}\cdot{D^{df}}\left(k\right)\cdot{\Gamma_{2}}\cdot{D^{ce}}\left({k+p}\right)}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{e{\varepsilon_{adc}}{{\left({2k+p}\right)}^{\mu}}\cdot{\delta^{df}}\cdot e{\varepsilon_{bef}}{{\left({2k+p}\right)}^{\nu}}{\delta^{ce}}}}{{\left({{k^{2}}-{m^{2}}}\right)\left[{{{\left({k+p}\right)}^{2}}-{m^{2}}}\right]}}

\displaystyle={e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{cda}}{\varepsilon_{cdb}}\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{{{\left({2k+p}\right)}^{\mu}}{{\left({2k+p}\right)}^{\nu}}}}{{\left({{k^{2}}-{m^{2}}}\right)\left[{{{\left({k+p}\right)}^{2}}-{m^{2}}}\right]}}

\displaystyle={e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{cda}}{\varepsilon_{cdb}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

(7.3.1)

Using
{\varepsilon_{cda}}{\varepsilon_{cdb}}=2{\delta_{ab}}

\displaystyle-i\pi_{ab1}^{\mu\nu}=2{e^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

(7.3.2)

We can make some definitions for aiding in the calculation:

\displaystyle N_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:={\left({2k+p}\right)^{\mu}}{\left({2k+p}\right)^{\nu}}

\displaystyle=4{k^{\mu}}{k^{\nu}}+2\left({{k^{\mu}}{p^{\nu}}+{p^{\mu}}{k^{\nu}}}\right)+{p^{\mu}}{p^{\nu}}

(7.3.3)

\displaystyle{D_{1}}

\displaystyle:={k^{2}}-{m^{2}}

(7.3.4)

\displaystyle{D_{2}}

\displaystyle:={\left({k+p}\right)^{2}}-{m^{2}}

\displaystyle={k^{2}}+2k\cdot p+{p^{2}}-{m^{2}}

\displaystyle={D_{1}}+2k\cdot p+{p^{2}}

(7.3.5)

So we have the relations

\displaystyle{k^{2}}

\displaystyle={D_{1}}+{m^{2}}

(7.3.6)

\displaystyle 2k\cdot p

\displaystyle={D_{2}}-{D_{1}}-{p^{2}}

(7.3.7)

The scalar product have now been expressed in terms of the denominators. We can split the integral over the transverse and longitudinal parts

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}

\displaystyle={f_{T1}}P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+{f_{L1}}P_{L}^{\mu\nu}

(7.3.8)

where the projectors are defined as

\displaystyle P_{L}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}

(7.3.9)

\displaystyle P_{T}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=g^{\mu\nu}-\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}

(7.3.10)

The coefficients can be extracted using the transverse and longitudinal projectors.

\displaystyle{P_{L\mu\nu}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={f_{L1}}

(7.3.11)

\displaystyle{P_{T\mu\nu}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\left({n-1}\right){f_{T1}}

(7.3.12)

The projectors acting on the numerator
N_{1}^{\mu\nu}
yield

\displaystyle{P_{L\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}{\left({2k\cdot p+{p^{2}}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}\left({D_{2}^{2}-2{D_{1}}{D_{2}}+D_{1}^{2}}\right)

(7.3.13)

and

\displaystyle{P_{T\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={g_{\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}-{P_{L\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle{g_{\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=4{k^{2}}+2k\cdot p+{p^{2}}

\displaystyle=2{D_{1}}+2{D_{2}}+4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}

(7.3.14)

The necessary ingredients have been assembled to extract the longitudinal coefficient

\displaystyle{f_{L1}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{{P_{L\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{D_{2}^{2}-2{D_{1}}{D_{2}}+D_{1}^{2}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\left({\frac{{{D_{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}}}+\frac{{{D_{1}}}}{{{D_{2}}}}-2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\left({\frac{{{D_{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}}}+\frac{{{D_{1}}}}{{{D_{2}}}}}\right)}

(7.3.15)

Since there are no massless particles in this field theory, we can safely make use of the Veltman conjecture by setting all dimensionless integrals to zero i.e.

\displaystyle\int\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\,k^{2\alpha}=0

(7.3.16)

for
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
. We are left with the task of evaluating

\displaystyle\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\frac{{{D_{1}}}}{{{D_{2}}}}}=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\frac{{{k^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}{{{{\left({k+p}\right)}^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}}

(7.3.17)

Making a change of variables

{l^{\mu}}=-{\left({k+p}\right)^{\mu}}\Rightarrow{k^{\mu}}=-{\left({l+p}\right)^{\mu}}\Rightarrow\displaystyle\int{\dfrac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}=\displaystyle\int{\dfrac{{{d^{n}}l}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}
. Substituting in the new measure

\displaystyle\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\frac{{{D_{1}}}}{{{D_{2}}}}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}l}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\frac{{{{\left({l+p}\right)}^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}{{{l^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\frac{{{D_{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}}}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\left({1+\frac{{2k\cdot p}}{{{k^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}+\frac{{{p^{2}}}}{{{k^{2}}-{m^{2}}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle={p^{2}}{A_{0}}\left(m\right)

(7.3.18)

where we have used the Passarino-Veltman scalar function
A_{0}(m)
(see Appendix
A
). So the longitudinal coefficient is

\displaystyle{f_{L1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{{p^{2}}}}\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}\left({\frac{{{D_{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}}}+\frac{{{D_{1}}}}{{{D_{2}}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)

(7.3.19)

The transverse coefficient can be evaluated as

\displaystyle\left({n-1}\right){f_{T1}}

\displaystyle={P_{T\mu\nu}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{{P_{T\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\left({\frac{{{g_{\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}-\frac{{{P_{L\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{2{D_{1}}+2{D_{2}}+4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}-\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\frac{{{P_{L\mu\nu}}N_{1}^{\mu\nu}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\int{\frac{{{d^{n}}k}}{{{{\left({2\pi}\right)}^{n}}}}}\left[{\frac{2}{{{D_{1}}}}+\frac{2}{{{D_{2}}}}+\frac{{4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}}{{{D_{1}}{D_{2}}}}}\right]-{f_{L1}}

\displaystyle=4{A_{0}}\left(m\right)+\left({4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({m;p,m}\right)-2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)

\displaystyle=2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)+\left({4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({m;p,m}\right)

\displaystyle\implies{f_{T1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{n-1}}\left[{2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)+\left({4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({m;p,m}\right)}\right]

(7.3.20)

So the integral
I_{1}^{\mu\nu}
is

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{n-1}}\left[{2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)+\left({4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({m;p,m}\right)}\right]P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}

(7.3.21)

with the self energy due to a pion bubble.

\displaystyle-i\pi_{ab1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=2{e^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}I_{1}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{{2{e^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}}}{{n-1}}\left[{2{A_{0}}\left(m\right)+\left({4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({m;p,m}\right)}\right]P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+4{e^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}{A_{0}}\left(m\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}

(7.3.22)

7.5
Summary of one loop vertices

We shall use these definitions for the calculations of the one loop corrections to the three point vertices:

\displaystyle{p^{\mu}}

\displaystyle=p_{1}^{\mu}+p_{2}^{\mu}

\displaystyle p\cdot\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)\cdot\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle{p_{1}}\cdot{p_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[{{{\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)}^{2}}-p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{p^{2}}-{m^{2}}

\displaystyle p\cdot{p_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)\cdot{p_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle p\cdot{p_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)\cdot{p_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{*{80}{c}}{p\cdot{p_{1}}=\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)\cdot{p_{1}}}&{p\cdot{p_{2}}=\left({{p_{1}}+{p_{2}}}\right)\cdot{p_{2}}}\\
{=\frac{1}{2}{p^{2}}}&{=\frac{1}{2}{p^{2}}}\end{array}

where
\{a,p_{1}\},\{b,p_{2}\}
are incoming momenta of the pions and
\{c,p^{\mu}\}
is the momentum of the outgoing rho.

Pion-Pion-Pion-Pion Vertex

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\phi}\left(s\right)

\displaystyle=2n{e^{4}}\left({2{\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}+{\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}+{\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({M;\sqrt{s},M}\right)

Including the Mandlestam
t
and
u
channels

\displaystyle V_{abcd}^{1\phi}\left({s,t,u}\right)

\displaystyle=2n{e^{4}}\Biggl[\left({2{\delta_{ab}}{\delta_{cd}}+{\delta_{ac}}{\delta_{bd}}+{\delta_{ad}}{\delta_{bc}}}\right){B_{0}}\left({M;\sqrt{s},M}\right)+

7.6
Renormalization

We shall use the On Shell Renormalization conditions to define the mass and residue of the pole which will fix the
renormalization constants. The conditions are

\displaystyle{\left.{\Pi\left({{p^{2}}}\right)}\right|_{{p^{2}}={m^{2}}}}

\displaystyle=0

(7.6.1)

\displaystyle{\left.{\frac{d}{{d{p^{2}}}}\Pi\left({{p^{2}}}\right)}\right|_{{p^{2}}={m^{2}}}}

\displaystyle=0

(7.6.2)

where
\Pi\left({{p^{2}}}\right)
represents the self energy, with the mass being set by condition (
7.6.1
) and the residue set with (
7.6.2
).

7.6.1
Rho Self Energy Renormalization

The counter term for the rho propagator can be written in terms of the projectors as

\displaystyle\bar{\Gamma}_{ab{\rm{CT}}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({{p^{2}}\delta{\bar{Z}_{A}}-\delta{\bar{M}^{2}}}\right){g^{\mu\nu}}+\left({\delta\bar{\xi}-\delta{\bar{Z}_{A}}}\right){k^{\mu}}{k^{\nu}}}\right]

\displaystyle=i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({{p^{2}}\delta{\bar{Z}_{A}}-\delta{\bar{M}^{2}}}\right)P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\left({{p^{2}}\delta\bar{\xi}-\delta{\bar{M}^{2}}}\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

(7.6.3)

The rho self energy is the sum of contributions including the symmetry factors from (
7.4.1
)-(
7.4.11
) and the counter term:

\displaystyle-i\pi_{ab}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{11}{\left[{-i\pi_{abj}^{\mu\nu}}\right]}+i{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({{p^{2}}\delta{\bar{Z}_{A}}-\delta{\bar{M}^{2}}}\right)P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\left({{p^{2}}\delta\bar{\xi}-\delta{\bar{M}^{2}}}\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}{\pi^{\mu\nu}}+\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({{p^{2}}\delta{Z_{A}}-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\left({{p^{2}}\delta\xi-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

(7.6.4)

\displaystyle:=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}{\delta_{ab}}\left(\pi_{T}P^{\mu\nu}_{T}+\pi_{L}P^{\mu\nu}_{L}\right)+\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}{\delta^{ab}}\left[{\left({{p^{2}}\delta{Z_{A}}-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\left({{p^{2}}\delta\xi-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

(7.6.5)

with

\displaystyle{\pi_{T}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{{65{e^{2}}{p^{2}}}}{{12\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[{{e^{2}}\left({2{m^{2}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+11{M^{2}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{2}}\right)+\frac{{12\kappa{m^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{2}}}}{2}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({-\frac{{2{e^{2}}}}{3}+\frac{{12{M^{2}}\kappa}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[m\right]+\left[{{e^{2}}\left({\frac{7}{6}+\frac{{9{M^{2}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right)+\frac{{3{M^{2}}{\lambda}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right]{A_{F}}\left[M\right]+\left({-\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{3}+\frac{{3{M^{2}}{\lambda}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{3}{e^{2}}{m^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{m,p,m}\right]+12{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{{13{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{p^{2}}}}\left({\frac{1}{{12}}\left({{M^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)\left({{A_{F}}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]-{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}\right)+\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right)+

\displaystyle+\frac{{12{m^{2}}{M^{2}}\kappa{\gamma_{e}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{3}{2}{M^{2}}\lambda{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{3{M^{4}}\lambda{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}\left({\frac{{8{m^{2}}}}{3}+\frac{{23{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{3{M^{4}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+2{m^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+11{M^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}{\gamma_{e}}}}{2}}\right)+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}{p^{2}}\left({-\frac{2}{3}{B_{F}}\left[{m,p,m}\right]+6{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]+\frac{1}{{12}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]-\frac{7}{{18}}+\frac{{65{\gamma_{e}}}}{{12}}}\right)

(7.6.6)

and

\displaystyle{\pi_{L}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{{{e^{2}}{p^{2}}}}{{2\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[{{e^{2}}\left({2{m^{2}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+11{M^{2}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{2}}\right)+\frac{{12\kappa{m^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{2}}}}{2}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({2{e^{2}}+\frac{{12{M^{2}}\kappa}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[m\right]+\left({{e^{2}}\left({9+\frac{{9{M^{2}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right)+\frac{{3{M^{2}}{\lambda}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[M\right]+\left({-\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{2}+\frac{{3{M^{2}}{\lambda}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right)A_{F}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+4{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]-2{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{p^{2}}}}\left({\frac{1}{4}\left({{M^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)\left({{A_{F}}\left[M\right]-{A_{F}}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]}\right)+\left({-\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{4}+\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{2}-\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{4}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right)+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}{p^{2}}\left({-\frac{1}{2}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]-\frac{{{\gamma_{e}}}}{2}}\right)+\frac{{12{m^{2}}{M^{2}}\kappa{\gamma_{e}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{3}{2}{M^{2}}{\lambda}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{3{M^{4}}{\lambda}{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}\left({-4{M^{2}}-\frac{{3{M^{4}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+2{m^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+11{M^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}{\gamma_{e}}}}{2}}\right)

(7.6.7)

where
\pi^{\mu\nu}
will in general have the form

\displaystyle{\pi^{\mu\nu}}=i\left({AP_{T}^{\mu\nu}+BP_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)

(7.6.8)

with

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{{{{\left({4\pi}\right)}^{2}}}}\left({{\pi_{T}}+{p^{2}}\delta{Z_{A}}-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)

(7.6.9)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{{{{\left({4\pi}\right)}^{2}}}}\left({{\pi_{L}}+{p^{2}}\delta\xi-\delta{M^{2}}}\right)

(7.6.10)

Applying the On Shell Renormalization conditions (
7.6.1
) and (
7.6.2
) to (
7.6.9
) and (
7.6.10
) we obtain
the renormalization constants:

\displaystyle-\delta{Z_{A}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{65{e^{2}}}}{{12\varepsilon}}-\frac{2}{3}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{m,M,m}\right]+6{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,M}\right]+\frac{1}{{12}}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{M^{4}}}}\left[{\frac{1}{{12}}\left({{M^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)\left({{A_{F}}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]-{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}\right)+\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({\frac{{8{e^{2}}{m^{2}}}}{3}-\frac{{2{e^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{3}}\right){\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{m,p,m}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+18{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,M}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+\left({\frac{{{e^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{{12}}+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{{13{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}}\right)+\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right)}\right){\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{7}{{18}}+\frac{{65{\gamma_{e}}}}{{12}}}\right)

(7.6.11)

\displaystyle\delta{M^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[{{e^{2}}\left({2{m^{2}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+11{M^{2}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{2}}\right)+\frac{{12\kappa{m^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{4}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda}{M^{2}}}}{2}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({-\frac{{2{e^{2}}}}{3}+\frac{{12{M^{2}}\kappa}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[m\right]+\left({{e^{2}}\left({\frac{7}{6}+\frac{{9{M^{2}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right)+\frac{{3{M^{2}}{\lambda}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}}\right){A_{F}}\left[M\right]+

\displaystyle+6{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,M}\right]-\frac{1}{{12}}{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{{13{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\frac{{2{e^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}\left[{\frac{1}{{12}}\left({{M^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)\left({{A_{F}}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]-{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}\right)+\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,{M^{2}},{m_{H}}}\right]}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({-\frac{8}{3}{e^{2}}{m^{2}}{M^{2}}+\frac{{2{e^{2}}{M^{4}}}}{3}}\right){\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{m,p,m}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}-18{e^{2}}{M^{4}}{\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,M}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+\left({-\frac{1}{{12}}{e^{2}}{M^{4}}-{e^{2}}{M^{2}}\left({-\frac{{13{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{m{h^{2}}}}{6}}\right)-{e^{2}}\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right)}\right){\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+{M^{2}}\left({-\frac{2}{3}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{m,M,m}\right]+6{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,M}\right]+\frac{1}{{12}}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}{M^{2}}\left({-\frac{7}{{18}}+\frac{{65{\gamma_{e}}}}{{12}}}\right)+\frac{{12{m^{2}}{M^{2}}\kappa{\gamma_{e}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{3}{2}{M^{2}}{\lambda}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{3{M^{4}}{\lambda}{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}-{e^{2}}{M^{2}}\left({-\frac{7}{{18}}+\frac{{65{\gamma_{e}}}}{{12}}}\right)+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}\left({\frac{{8{m^{2}}}}{3}+\frac{{23{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{3{M^{4}}}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+2{m^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+11{M^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{9{M^{4}}{\gamma_{e}}}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}{\gamma_{e}}}}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-\delta\xi

\displaystyle=-\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{2\varepsilon}}-\frac{2}{3}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{m,M,m}\right]+6{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,M}\right]+\frac{1}{{12}}{e^{2}}{B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]+

\displaystyle-\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{M^{4}}}}\left({\frac{1}{{12}}\left({{M^{2}}-m_{H}^{2}}\right)\left({A_{F}\left[{{m_{H}}}\right]-A_{F}\left[M\right]}\right)+\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,M,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right)+

\displaystyle+\left({\frac{{8{e^{2}}{m^{2}}}}{3}-\frac{{2{e^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{3}}\right){\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{m,p,m}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+18{e^{2}}{M^{2}}{\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,M}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+\left[{\frac{{{e^{2}}{M^{2}}}}{{12}}+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{{13{M^{2}}}}{6}-\frac{{m_{H}^{2}}}{6}}\right)+\frac{{{e^{2}}}}{{{M^{2}}}}\left({\frac{{{M^{4}}}}{{12}}-\frac{{{M^{2}}m_{H}^{2}}}{6}+\frac{{m_{H}^{4}}}{{12}}}\right)}\right]{\left.{\frac{\partial}{{\partial p}}B_{F}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]}\right|_{p=M}}+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}\left({-\frac{7}{{18}}+\frac{{65{\gamma_{e}}}}{{12}}}\right)

(7.6.13)

The contributions from infinitely many self energy insertions leads to a geometric series which can be summed with
D:={p^{2}}-{M^{2}}+i\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle iD_{ab}^{(1)\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i}}{D}{\delta_{ab}}\left({P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}{\frac{{-i}}{D}{\delta_{ab}}\left({P_{T}^{\mu{\alpha_{1}}}+P_{L}^{\mu{\alpha_{1}}}}\right)\prod\limits_{k=1}^{n}{\left[{i\pi_{{\alpha_{2k-1}}}^{{\alpha_{2k}}}\frac{{-i}}{D}\left({P_{T{\alpha_{2k}}}^{{\alpha_{2k+1}}}+P_{L{\alpha_{2k}}}^{{\alpha_{2k+1}}}}\right)}\right]}}\left[{i\pi_{{\alpha_{2n+1}}}^{{\alpha_{2n+2}}}\frac{{-i}}{D}\left({P_{T{\alpha_{2n+2}}}^{\nu}+P_{L{\alpha_{2n+2}}}^{\nu}}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i}}{D}{\delta_{ab}}\left({P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)+\frac{{-i}}{D}{\delta_{ab}}\left({P_{T}^{\mu{\alpha_{1}}}+P_{L}^{\mu{\alpha_{1}}}}\right)\cdot i\left({AP_{T{\alpha_{1}}}^{{\alpha_{2}}}+BP_{L{\alpha_{1}}}^{{\alpha_{2}}}}\right)\cdot\frac{{-i}}{D}\left({P_{T{\alpha_{2}}}^{\nu}+P_{L{\alpha_{2}}}^{\nu}}\right)+\cdots

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i}}{D}{\delta_{ab}}\left({P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)+\frac{{-i}}{{{D^{2}}}}{\delta_{ab}}\left({AP_{T}^{\mu\nu}+BP_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)+\frac{{-i}}{{{D^{3}}}}{\delta_{ab}}\left({{A^{2}}P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+{B^{2}}P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right)+\cdots

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i{\delta_{ab}}}}{D}\left\{{\left[{1+\frac{A}{D}+{{\left({\frac{A}{D}}\right)}^{2}}+\cdots}\right]P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\left[{1+\frac{B}{D}+{{\left({\frac{B}{D}}\right)}^{2}}+\cdots}\right]P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i{\delta_{ab}}}}{D}\left[{\frac{1}{{1-\frac{A}{D}}}P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{{1-\frac{B}{D}}}P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i{\delta_{ab}}}}{{D-A}}\left[{P_{T}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{{D-A}}{{D-B}}P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{{-i{\delta_{ab}}}}{{D-A}}\left[{{g^{\mu\nu}}+\frac{{B-A}}{{D-B}}P_{L}^{\mu\nu}}\right]

\displaystyle\cong\frac{{-i{\delta_{ab}}}}{{D-A}}g^{\mu\nu}

(7.6.14)

Since the longitudinal projector is made up of on shell momenta which will always encounter the on shell momentum from the Feynman vertices, the longitudinal piece of the one loop propagator will not contribute to scattering amplitudes.

7.6.2

The counter term for the Higgs propagator is

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}_{\rm{CT}}=i\left({{k^{2}}\delta{\bar{Z}_{H}}-\delta{\bar{m}_{H}^{2}}}\right)

The of Higgs self energy diagrams from (

with

\displaystyle+\frac{{{M^{2}}}}{{{e^{2}}}}\left({48{\kappa^{2}}-\frac{{18{m^{2}}\kappa\lambda}}{{m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{{3{\lambda^{2}}}}{4}-\frac{{9{M^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}}}{{4m_{H}^{2}}}}\right)\Biggr]+\frac{{9{M^{4}}\lambda}}{{2m_{H}^{2}}}+3{m^{2}}\kappa{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{3}{8}{M^{2}}\lambda{\gamma_{e}}+

\displaystyle-\frac{{27{M^{4}}\lambda{\gamma_{e}}}}{{4m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{3}{4}m_{H}^{2}\lambda{\gamma_{e}}+3\kappa{A_{F}}\left[m\right]+\frac{3}{8}\lambda{A_{F}}\left[M\right]-\frac{{27{M^{2}}\lambda{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}}{{4m_{H}^{2}}}+\frac{3}{4}\lambda{A_{F}}\left[{mh}\right]+

\displaystyle+{e^{2}}{p^{2}}\left({-\frac{{3{\gamma_{e}}}}{4}-\frac{3}{4}{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]}\right)+

7.6.3
Pion-Pion-Rho Vertex Renormalization

For the vertex renormalization, we shall resort to using the Minimal Subtraction scheme. A conventional condition is to define the coupling at zero transferred three momentum. This is difficult to impose since the three point scalar function
C_{0}
is difficult to manipulate. Summing up the vertex corrections (
7.5.1
) to (
7.5.10
)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{10}{V_{k}^{\mu}}=\frac{i}{{{{\left({4\pi}\right)}^{2}}}}{\varepsilon_{cab}}{\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{2}}}\right)^{\mu}}\left({i{V_{e}}}\right)

(7.6.20)

where
V_{e}
is defined as

\displaystyle{V_{e}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{{{e^{3}}}}{{2\varepsilon}}+{e^{3}}\left({-\frac{{3{A_{F}}\left[m\right]}}{{2{m^{2}}}}+\frac{{3{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}}{{2{m^{2}}}}+\left({\frac{9}{2}-\frac{{3{M^{2}}}}{{2{m^{2}}}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{m,m,M}\right]+3{\gamma_{e}}}\right)+\frac{1}{{4{m^{2}}-{p^{2}}}}\times\Biggl\{

\displaystyle e{\kappa^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left({32{B_{F}}\left[{m,m,{m_{H}}}\right]-32{B_{F}}\left[{m,p,m}\right]+\left({64{m^{2}}-32m_{H}^{2}-16{p^{2}}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{{m_{H}},{p_{1}},m,-{p_{2}},m}\right]}\right)+

\displaystyle+{e^{3}}\biggl[\left({6-\frac{{3{p^{2}}}}{{2{m^{2}}}}}\right)\left({{A_{F}}\left[m\right]-{A_{F}}\left[M\right]}\right)+\left({-24{m^{2}}+10{M^{2}}+9{p^{2}}-\frac{{3{M^{2}}{p^{2}}}}{{2{m^{2}}}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{m,m,M}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({8{m^{2}}-2{M^{2}}-4{p^{2}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{m,p,m}\right]+\left({-2{M^{2}}-{p^{2}}}\right){B_{F}}\left[{M,p,M}\right]+

\displaystyle+\left({-8{m^{2}}{M^{2}}+2{M^{4}}-4{m^{2}}{p^{2}}+2{M^{2}}{p^{2}}+\frac{{{p^{4}}}}{2}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{m,{p_{1}},M,-{p_{2}},M}\right]+\kappa{\nu^{2}}\bigg(-4{B_{F}}\left[{m,m,M}\right]+

\displaystyle-4{B_{F}}\left[{m,m,{m_{H}}}\right]+8{B_{F}}\left[{M,p,{m_{H}}}\right]+\left({32{m^{2}}-4{M^{2}}-4m_{H}^{2}-4{p^{2}}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{m,{p_{1}},M,-{p_{2}},{m_{H}}}\right]\bigg)+

\displaystyle\left({-16{m^{4}}+12{m^{2}}{M^{2}}-2{M^{4}}+12{m^{2}}{p^{2}}-5{M^{2}}{p^{2}}-2{p^{4}}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{m,{p_{2}},M,p,m}\right]-10{m^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}+\frac{{5{p^{2}}{\gamma_{e}}}}{2}\biggr]\Biggr\}

The one loop vertex correction including the counter term for the pion-pion-rho vertex correction is

\displaystyle{e^{\left(1\right)}}{\varepsilon_{cab}}{\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{2}}}\right)^{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{10}{V_{k}^{\mu}}+\delta{\overline{Z}_{e}}{\varepsilon_{cab}}{\left({{p_{1}}-{p_{2}}}\right)^{\mu}}

with
{e^{\left(1\right)}}
the finite one loop correction to the coupling. Comparing (

) with (

) we can read off the renormalization constant:

\displaystyle\delta{Z_{e}}:=\frac{{{e^{3}}}}{{2\varepsilon}}

(7.6.23)

7.6.4

\displaystyle\left({-32{m^{2}}+8{M^{2}}+4{p^{2}}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{m,{p_{1}},M,-{p_{2}},M}\right]+\left({-32{m^{2}}+8{M^{2}}+16{p^{2}}}\right){C_{0}}\left[{M,{p_{1}},m,-{p_{2}},m}\right]\Biggr]

\displaystyle iV_{H}^{\left(1\right)}{\delta_{ab}}

Comparing the expressions (

) with (
7.6.26
) we can read off the renormalization constant

\displaystyle\delta{Z_{H}}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left({16{e^{4}}\nu-16{e^{2}}\kappa\nu+16{\kappa^{2}}\nu+6\kappa\lambda\nu+20\kappa\nu{\lambda_{4}}}\right)

where

Matching coefficients between (

) and (
7.6.29
), we see the value of the counter term is

\displaystyle\delta Z_{\phi^{4}}:=\left({40{e^{4}}+16{\kappa^{2}}+2{e^{2}}\lambda 4+11\lambda{4^{4}}}\right)\frac{1}{\varepsilon}

(7.6.30)

We have completed the renormalization of all the 1PI diagrams that can contribute to pion-pion scattering processes.

8
Conclusion and Outlook

𝕎
e set off on a journey to extend the Kroll-Lee-Zumino model which only had as its particle content the charged pions and neutral rho. This was done by using the larger gauge group SU(2). This group allowed for the inclusion of a larger particle content with the charged and neutral pions and rhos. Though there was a price to be paid in the form of simplicity. Whereas for the Kroll-Lee-Zumino model, the neutral rho mass could be included externally by hand without breaking the U(1) gauge invariance, such a procedure in the SU(2) extension was not possible as it breaks the gauge invariance of the theory. The predictive power of gauge theories were too alluring to sacrifice and this forced us to generate the mass for the rho via Spontaneous Symmetry breaking using the Higgs mechanism while preserving the gauge invariance.

We went on to calculate the pion-pion scattering amplitudes. The scattering lengths
a
and
b
were then computed with values summarized in the table
6.1
, listed below for convenience:
